An Explicit Vinogradov–Korobov Zero-Free Region for Dirichlet L \mathchar 29004 -functions
Tanmay Khale

Abstract
. We establish the first explicit form of the Vinogradov–Korobov zero-free region for Dirichlet L \mathchar 29004 -functions.



1.
Introduction and statement of main results

[

[

\mathchar 29027\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720

Montgomery

states this result without reference or proof. A zero-free region of the form (

[

\mathchar 29004
-functions of Hecke characters over a number field
\mathchar 29003

\mathchar 29003\mathchar 12349\mathbb{\mathchar 29009}
. For completeness, we give a short proof of (
1.4
) in in Appendix
B
that relies only on well-known bounds for the sum in (
2.5
) along with a convenient version of Jensen’s formula from complex analysis.

2.1.
Preliminaries on Dirichlet
\mathchar 29004
-functions

The results in this subsection can be found in
[
3
]
. Let
\mathchar 28959\pmod{\mathchar 29041}
be a Dirichlet character. The Dirichlet
\mathchar 29004
-function
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
is defined by the absolutely convergent Dirichlet series

\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 561}{{\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785\over\mathchar 29038^{\mathchar 29043}}}

in the half-plane
\mathrm{\mathchar 29010\mathchar 29029}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28721
. If
\mathchar 28959
is nonprincipal, then
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
extends to an entire function, while if
\mathchar 28959
is principal, then
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
extends to a meromorphic function on the complex plane with a single simple pole at
\mathchar 29043\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
.

We say that
\mathchar 28959
is odd (resp. even) if
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721
(resp.
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
). We write

\mathfrak{\mathchar 29025}{\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785}\mathchar 12349\begin{cases}\mathchar 28720&\text{ when }\mathchar 28959\text{ is even,}\\
\mathchar 28721&\text{ when }\mathchar 28959\text{ is odd.}\end{cases}

When there is no ambiguity concerning the Dirichlet character under consideration, we write
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29025}
instead of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29025}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
. We also write

\mathchar 28942\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\begin{cases}\mathchar 28720&\text{ when }\mathchar 28959\text{ is nonprincipal,}\\
\mathchar 28721&\text{ when }\mathchar 28959\text{ is principal.}\end{cases}

Given
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
we define the
completed

-function

by

Observe that since
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 28959}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\overline{\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\overline{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785}

is free of zeros of
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\mathchar 24891\overline{\mathchar 28959}\delimiter 84054785

is also zero-free. It therefore suffices to demonstrate a zero-free region holding for arbitrary
\mathchar 28959
and
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720
to conclude a zero-free region holding for arbitrary
\mathchar 28959
and
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785
positive or negative. Throughout the remainder of the paper we will impose the restriction
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29043\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720
without loss of generality. By McCurley’s
[
12
, Theorem 1.1]
explicit version of a classical zero-free region of de la Vallée Poussin,
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28955\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29044\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
is non-vanishing for
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720
and

(2.3)

\mathchar 28955\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28729\mathchar 314\mathchar 28726\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28729\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28728\mathchar 28728\mathchar 28720\log(\max\{q,qt,10\})}}\mathchar 314

apart from a possible real zero which is necessarily simple.

General Results

Theorem

is a consequence of the following more general result.

.

From the preceding lemma, we immediately obtain the following corollary.

Corollary A.2
.

If

\displaystyle\quad\mathchar 8235\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 314\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28725\log_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28723\mathchar 314\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28726\mathchar 28723\log(A+1)\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723\mathchar 314\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28994^{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28723}\Big\delimiter 67273472{{\log\mathchar 28941\over\log\log\mathchar 28941}}\Big\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891

meaning that

{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28940}}\mathchar 12820\Big\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28726\mathchar 8235{{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 314\mathchar 28728\mathchar 28729\log_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28941\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28724\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28724\log(A+1)\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28729\mathchar 28725\over\log\log\mathchar 28941}}\Big\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28994^{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\log\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\log\log\mathchar 28941\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28721\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28727\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28729\log\mathchar 29041\mathchar 24891

a contradiction.
∎

Corollary B.2
.

Let
\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723
, and let
\mathchar 28959\pmod{\mathchar 29041}
be a Dirichlet character. Then,
\mathchar 29004\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28955\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29044\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28959\delimiter 84054785
is nonvanishing in the regions

\displaystyle\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28728\log\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28728\mathchar 28726\delimiter 67273472\log\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29044\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28722\over\mathchar 28723}}}\delimiter 67273472\log\log\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29044\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28723}}}}}\mathchar 24891\hskip 18.49988pt\absolutevalue{t}\mathchar 12821\exp(\exp(\exp(23)))\mathchar 24891

\displaystyle\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28728\log\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28724\delimiter 67273472\log\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29044\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28722\over\mathchar 28723}}}\delimiter 67273472\log\log\delimiter 69640972\mathchar 29044\delimiter 69640972\delimiter 84054785^{{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28723}}}}}\mathchar 24891\hskip 18.49988pt\absolutevalue{t}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28723\mathchar 314

Proof.

The first part follows from Theorem
B.1
with
\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28725
. For the second part, we take
\mathchar 28993\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28726\mathchar 314\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 28994\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28725
. If
\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 29029^{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720}
, then
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\mathchar 314\mathchar 28728\mathchar 28729\log_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 29044\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28724\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28725\log(A+1)\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723\mathchar 314\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28729\mathchar 28725\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28729\mathchar 314\mathchar 28728
. Thus, by Theorem
B.1
, we obtain
\mathchar 29027\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28726\mathchar 8235{{\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28729\mathchar 314\mathchar 28728\over\log\log\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28720}}}
. The desired result follows once we take
\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29029^{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720}
and apply (
2.3
) for
\mathchar 29044\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29012_{\mathchar 28720}
.
∎
